<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

To make porridge, Goldilocks mixes $3$ bags of oats with $1$ bag containing $20\%$ wheat bran and $80\%$ oats. 
All the bags have the same volume. What percentage of the porridge mix is wheat bran?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage} &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{20}{100} \times \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5 \times 4} \times 100  \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 }{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {100} {20} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times 5}{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                  &= 5 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage} &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{20}{100} \times \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5 \times 4} \times 100  \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 }{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {100} {20} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times 5}{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                  &= 5 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

When a glass is full of water the total mass is $400 \ \text{g}$. 
When the glass is half full of water the total mass is $320 \ \text{g}$. Find the mass of the glass.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the mass of water $= w$

Let the mass of glass $= g$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
w + g                                              &= 400 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + g                                   &= 320 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + \dfrac{g \times 2}{1 \times 2}      &= 320 \\\\
w + \dfrac{2g}{2}                                  &= 320 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 320 \times 2 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 720
\end {aligned}
$

We have got 2 variables and we have 2 equations to solve them. Lets eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.

Subtract the first equation from the second to reduce it to one variable.

$
\begin {aligned}
w + 2g - w - g                &= 720 - 400 \\\\
g                             &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end {aligned}
$

Mass of glass $= 240 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of half water} &= 400 - 320 \\\\
                          &= 80 \\\\
\text{Mass of water}      &= 2 \times 80 \\\\
                          &= 160 \\\\
\text{Mass of glass}      &= \text{Total mass} - \text{Mass of water} \\\\
                          &= 400 -  160 \\\\
                          &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$240 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the mass of water $= w$
<emptyline>
Let the mass of glass $= g$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
w + g                                              &= 400 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + g                                   &= 320 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + \dfrac{g \times 2}{1 \times 2}      &= 320 \\\\
w + \dfrac{2g}{2}                                  &= 320 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 320 \times 2 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 720
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got 2 variables and we have 2 equations to solve them. Lets eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.
<emptyline>
Subtract the first equation from the second to reduce it to one variable.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
w + 2g - w - g                &= 720 - 400 \\\\
g                             &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mass of glass $= 240 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of half water} &= 400 - 320 \\\\
                          &= 80 \\\\
\text{Mass of water}      &= 2 \times 80 \\\\
                          &= 160 \\\\
\text{Mass of glass}      &= \text{Total mass} - \text{Mass of water} \\\\
                          &= 400 -  160 \\\\
                          &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$3x = 36$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3x &= 36 \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
x  &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Fazal has $\pounds 2.60$ and Gareth has $\pounds 5.30$. Gareth gives Fazal some $5 \text{p}$ coins. 
Each boy now has the same amount of money. How many $5 \text{p}$ coins did Gareth give Fazal?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let number of coins $= a$

Money given $= a \times 5 \text{p}$ 

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 5.3 - 5 \text{p} \times a                     &= \pounds 2.6 + 5\text{p} \times a \\\\
5.3 \times 100\text{p} - 5\text{p} \times a           &= 2.6 \times 100\text{p} + 5\text{p} \times a \\\\
530 \text{p} - 5 \text{p} \times a                    &= 260\text{p} + 5\text{p} \times a \\\\
530 - 5 \times a                                      &= 260 + 5 \times a \\\\
530 - 5a                                              &= 260 + 5a \\\\
530 - 260                                             &= 5a + 5a \\\\
5a + 5a                                               &= 530 - 260 \\\\
10a                                                   &= 270 \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{270}{10} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{27\cancel{0}{1\cancel{0}} \\\\
a                                                     &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

Number of coins = 27

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let number of coins $= a$
<emptyline>
Money given $= a \times 5 \text{p}$ 
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 5.3 - 5 \text{p} \times a                     &= \pounds 2.6 + 5\text{p} \times a \\\\
5.3 \times 100\text{p} - 5\text{p} \times a           &= 2.6 \times 100\text{p} + 5\text{p} \times a \\\\
530 \text{p} - 5 \text{p} \times a                    &= 260\text{p} + 5\text{p} \times a \\\\
530 - 5 \times a                                      &= 260 + 5 \times a \\\\
530 - 5a                                              &= 260 + 5a \\\\
530 - 260                                             &= 5a + 5a \\\\
5a + 5a                                               &= 530 - 260 \\\\
10a                                                   &= 270 \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{270}{10} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{27\cancel{0}{1\cancel{0}} \\\\
a                                                     &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of coins = 27
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

If: $\dfrac{3}{x} \times \dfrac{4}{7} = \dfrac{3}{14}$ 

Calculate the value of $x$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {x} \times \dfrac {4} {7}  &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {3 \times 4} {x \times 7}      &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {x}                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3 \times 4 \times 14} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 14} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7 \times 2} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {7}} \\\\  
x                                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {x} \times \dfrac {4} {7}  &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {3 \times 4} {x \times 7}      &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {x}                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3 \times 4 \times 14} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 14} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7 \times 2} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {7}} \\\\  
x                                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

There are $x$ horses, $y$ cows and one three legged dog in a field.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down an expression in algebra for the total number of legs in the field.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of legs} &= x \times 4 + y \times 4 + 3 \\\\
                            &= 4x + 4y + 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4x + 4y + 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of legs} &= x \times 4 + y \times 4 + 3 \\\\
                            &= 4x + 4y + 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4x + 4y + 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Half of the cows and one quarter of the horses leave the field. 
Write down an expression in algebra for the number of heads that remain in the field.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of heads}                        &= x + y + 1 - ( \dfrac{1}{2} \times x ) - ( \dfrac{1}{4} \times y ) \\\\
                                                    &= x - \dfrac{x}{2} + y - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x \times 2}{1 \times 2} - \dfrac{x \times 1}{2 \times 1} + \dfrac{y \times 4}{1 \times 4} - \dfrac{y \times 1}{4 \times 1} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x}{2} - \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{4y}{4} - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x-x}{2} + \dfrac{4y - y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of heads}                        &= x + y + 1 - ( \dfrac{1}{2} \times x ) - ( \dfrac{1}{4} \times y ) \\\\
                                                    &= x - \dfrac{x}{2} + y - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x \times 2}{1 \times 2} - \dfrac{x \times 1}{2 \times 1} + \dfrac{y \times 4}{1 \times 4} - \dfrac{y \times 1}{4 \times 1} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x}{2} - \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{4y}{4} - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x-x}{2} + \dfrac{4y - y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

The number of leaf cutter ants doubles every day for the first week.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If there were $10$ at the end of the first day how many are there at the end of the second day?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Ants at the end of the second day} &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
&= 20 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

20

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Ants at the end of the second day} &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
&= 20 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>20
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many were there at the end of the week?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

| Day | Ants                  |
|-----|-----------------------|
| 1   | $2 \times 10 = 20$    |
| 2   | $2 \times 20 = 40$    |
| 3   | $2 \times 40 = 80$    |
| 4   | $2 \times 80 = 160$   |
| 5   | $2 \times 160 = 320$  |
| 6   | $2 \times 320 = 640$  |
| 7   | $2 \times 640 = 1280$ |

Ants at the end of the week $= 1280$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

1280

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>| Day | Ants                  |
|-----|-----------------------|
| 1   | $2 \times 10 = 20$    |
| 2   | $2 \times 20 = 40$    |
| 3   | $2 \times 40 = 80$    |
| 4   | $2 \times 80 = 160$   |
| 5   | $2 \times 160 = 320$  |
| 6   | $2 \times 320 = 640$  |
| 7   | $2 \times 640 = 1280$ |
<emptyline>
Ants at the end of the week $= 1280$ 
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>1280
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A "double-decker" sandwich has three slices of bread and two layers of filling. 
(e.g. bread/filling/bread/filling/bread). 
Each slice of bread has to be buttered on each side that is in contact with the filling. 
I make as many of these sandwiches as possible from a sliced loaf which has $22$ usable slices, 
excluding crusts which are not used. How many sides of bread do I have to butter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

For every sandwich, $4$ sides of bread need to be buttered.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sandwiches}    &= 22 \div 3 \\\\
                               &= 7 \ \ \text{remainder} \ \ 1 \\\\
                               &\approx 7 \\\\
\text{Number of sides}         &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
                               &= 28  
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>For every sandwich, $4$ sides of bread need to be buttered.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sandwiches}    &= 22 \div 3 \\\\
                               &= 7 \ \ \text{remainder} \ \ 1 \\\\
                               &\approx 7 \\\\
\text{Number of sides}         &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
                               &= 28  
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$28$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Jenn's fish tank was filled half way. She added $9 \ \text{gallons}$ of water to the tank and found that it was then $\dfrac{7}{8}$ full. 

How much water can the tank hold altogether?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the water tank hold together $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{7}{8} \times a                                   &= 9 + \dfrac{a}{2} \\\\
\dfrac{7}{8} \times a - \dfrac{a}{2}                    &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{7 \times a}{8} - \dfrac{a \times 4}{2 \times 4}  &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{7 \times a}{8} - \dfrac{a \times 4}{2 \times 4}  &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{7a}{8} - \dfrac{4a}{8}                           &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{3a}{8}                                           &= 9 \\\\
3a                                                      &= 9 \times 8 \\\\
3a                                                      &= 72 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{72}{3} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{24 \times 3}{3} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{24 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
a                                                       &= 24 \ \text{gallons}
\end{aligned}
$

Water the tank can hold $= 24 \ \text{gallons}$  

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24 \ \text{gallons}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the water tank hold together $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{7}{8} \times a                                   &= 9 + \dfrac{a}{2} \\\\
\dfrac{7}{8} \times a - \dfrac{a}{2}                    &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{7 \times a}{8} - \dfrac{a \times 4}{2 \times 4}  &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{7 \times a}{8} - \dfrac{a \times 4}{2 \times 4}  &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{7a}{8} - \dfrac{4a}{8}                           &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac{3a}{8}                                           &= 9 \\\\
3a                                                      &= 9 \times 8 \\\\
3a                                                      &= 72 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{72}{3} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{24 \times 3}{3} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{24 \times \cancel{3}}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
a                                                       &= 24 \ \text{gallons}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Water the tank can hold $= 24 \ \text{gallons}$  
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24 \ \text{gallons}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Two girls were on a sponsored walk from Pangbourne to Goring.

One of them gave up $5 \ \text{km}$ after she had passed the half-way check-point.

She was then $10 \ \text{km}$ from Goring.

How far apart are the towns?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the distance between towns $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 10                                          &= \dfrac{a}{2} + 5 \\\\ 
a - \dfrac{a}{2}                                &= 5 + 10 \\\\ 
\dfrac{a \times 2}{1 \times 2}  - \dfrac{a}{2}  &= 15 \\\\ 
\dfrac{2a}{2}  - \dfrac{a}{2}                   &= 15 \\\\ 
\dfrac{2a - a}{2}                               &= 15 \\\\ 
\dfrac{a}{2}                                    &= 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

Distance between two towns $= 30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the distance between towns $= a$
<emptyline>

\dfrac{2a - a}{2}                               &= 15 \\\\ 
\dfrac{a}{2}                                    &= 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \times 2 \\\\
a                                               &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Distance between two towns $= 30$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$30$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Mrs Jones prints books.

![north-london-collegiate-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-20-00.png](/assets/north-london-collegiate-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-20-00.png "north-london-collegiate-school--11-plus--maths--9999--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-20-00.png")

Jon pays $\bold{\pounds 4.35}$ for his book, **including the cover**.

How many **pages** are in his book?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of pages $=a$

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times a + 75 \text{p} &= \pounds 4.35 \\\\
3 \times a + 75 \text{p} &= 4.35 \times 100 \text{p} \\\\
3 \times a + 75          &= 435 \\\\
3 \times a               &= 435 - 75 \\\\
3 \times a               &= 360 \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {360} {3} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {120 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {120 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                        &= 120
\end{aligned}
$

Number of pages in the book = $120$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of pages $=a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$

a                        &= \dfrac {360} {3} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {120 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {120 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                        &= 120
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of pages in the book = $120$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A boy ate $100$ cookies in $\text{five}$ days. Each day he ate $6$ more then the day before. How many cookies did he eat on the first day?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the cookies ate on first day $= a$

As per the question

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 4 \times 6  &= 100 \\\\
a + 24          &= 100 \\\\
a               &= 100 - 24 \\\\
a               &= 76
\end{aligned}
$

Cookies he ate on first day $= 76$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$76$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the cookies ate on first day $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + 4 \times 6  &= 100 \\\\
a + 24          &= 100 \\\\
a               &= 100 - 24 \\\\
a               &= 76
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cookies he ate on first day $= 76$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

It takes two decorators $8$ days to paint a house.

One is lazy and one is energetic.

The energetic one could paint the house in $12$ days on his own.

How many days would it take the lazy one to paint the house on his own?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let time taken by lazy person $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{1}{8}                                                        &= \dfrac{1}{12} + \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1}{8} - \dfrac{1}{12}                                        &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1 \times 3}{8 \times 3} - \dfrac{1 \times 2}{12 \times 2}    &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{3}{24} - \dfrac{2}{24}                                       &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{3-2}{24}                                                     &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1}{24}                                                       &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1 \times a}{24}                                              &= 1  \\\\
\dfrac{a}{24}                                                       &= 1  \\\\
a                                                                   &= 24\ \text{days}
\end{aligned}
$

Time taken by lazy person to paint a house $= 24$ days


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let time taken by lazy person $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{1}{8}                                                        &= \dfrac{1}{12} + \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1}{8} - \dfrac{1}{12}                                        &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1 \times 3}{8 \times 3} - \dfrac{1 \times 2}{12 \times 2}    &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{3}{24} - \dfrac{2}{24}                                       &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{3-2}{24}                                                     &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1}{24}                                                       &= \dfrac{1}{a}  \\\\
\dfrac{1 \times a}{24}                                              &= 1  \\\\
\dfrac{a}{24}                                                       &= 1  \\\\
a                                                                   &= 24\ \text{days}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Time taken by lazy person to paint a house $= 24$ days
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Three ducks and two ducklings weigh $32 \ \text{kg}$. Four ducks and three ducklings weigh $44 \ \text{kg}$. All ducks weigh the same as each other and all ducklings weigh the same as each other.

What is the weight of two ducks and one duckling?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of the duck $= a$

Let the weight of the duckling $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2a + 2b                      &= 32 \\\\
4a + 3b                      &= 44
\end{aligned}
$

Subtracting the first equation from the second, we get

$
\begin{aligned}
4a + 3b - 2a - 2b                           &= 44 - 32 \\\\
2a + 1b                                     &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Weight of two ducks and one duckling $= 12 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of the duck $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the weight of the duckling $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2a + 2b                      &= 32 \\\\
4a + 3b                      &= 44
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Subtracting the first equation from the second, we get
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4a + 3b - 2a - 2b                           &= 44 - 32 \\\\
2a + 1b                                     &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of two ducks and one duckling $= 12 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$3.49 \times \ldots = 349000$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $=a$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a                               &= \dfrac {349000} {3.49} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {349000 \times 100} {3.49 \times 100} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {349 \times 1000 \times 100}{349} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel{349} \times 1000 \times 100}{\cancel{349}} \\\\
a                               &= 1000 \times 100 \\\\
a                               &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

a                               &= \dfrac {349 \times 1000 \times 100}{349} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel{349} \times 1000 \times 100}{\cancel{349}} \\\\
a                               &= 1000 \times 100 \\\\
a                               &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$9 \div   \ldots = 36$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the missing number $=a$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
9 \div a                                 &= 36 \\\\
9 \times \dfrac{1}{a}                    &= 36 \\\\
\dfrac{9}{a}                             &= 36 \\\\
9                                        &= 36 \times a \\\\
36 \times a                              &= 9 \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac{9}{36} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {9} {9 \times 4} \\\\
                                         &= \dfrac{\cancel{9}} {\cancel{9} \times 4} \\\\
                                         &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>

a                                        &= \dfrac{9}{36} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {9} {9 \times 4} \\\\
                                         &= \dfrac{\cancel{9}} {\cancel{9} \times 4} \\\\
                                         &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Five friends shared the cost of a mansion (priced at $3$ million pounds) equally. How much did each of them pay ?  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the money each friend pay $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + a + a + a       &= 3000000 \\\\
5a                      &= 3000000 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3000000} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {600000 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
a                       &= \pounds 600000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 600000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the money each friend pay $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + a + a + a       &= 3000000 \\\\
5a                      &= 3000000 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3000000} {5} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {600000 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
a                       &= \pounds 600000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 600000$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Robert enjoyed his summer holiday. He spent half of it working in a music store. He spent half of the remaining time reading and relaxing at home, and, after spending $4$ days at a friend’s house, he had exactly $2$ weeks left to attend a summer camp. How long (in days) was his summer holiday ?  

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of days in summer holiday $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{1}{2}(\dfrac{1}{2} \times d)     &= 4 + 2 \times 7 \\\\
\dfrac{1 \times 1 \times d}{2 \times 2} &= 4 + 2 \times 7 \\\\
\dfrac{d}{4}                            &= 4 + 14 \\\\
\dfrac{d}{4}                            &= 18 \\\\
d                                       &= 18 \times 4 \\\\
d                                       &= 72 \ \text{days}
\end{aligned}
$

Robert summer holiday was $72$ days long.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72 \ \text{days}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of days in summer holiday $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac{1}{2}(\dfrac{1}{2} \times d)     &= 4 + 2 \times 7 \\\\
\dfrac{1 \times 1 \times d}{2 \times 2} &= 4 + 2 \times 7 \\\\
\dfrac{d}{4}                            &= 4 + 14 \\\\
\dfrac{d}{4}                            &= 18 \\\\
d                                       &= 18 \times 4 \\\\
d                                       &= 72 \ \text{days}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Robert summer holiday was $72$ days long.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$72 \ \text{days}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

![oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-22-00.png](/assets/oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-22-00.png "oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-22-00.png")   ![oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-22-01.png](/assets/oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-22-01.png "oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-22-01.png")

As his hobby, Fred builds $3$-legged stools and $4$-legged tables. Last month he used $72$ legs and he built $3$ more stools than tables. How many stools and how many tables did he build ? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of tables $= a$

Number of stools $= a + 3$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a + 3 \times (a + 3)           &= 72 \\\\
4a + 3a + 9                             &= 72 \\\\
7a + 9                                  &= 72 \\\\
7a                                      &= 72 - 9 \\\\
7a                                      &= 63 \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac{63}{7} \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac{9 \times 7}{7} \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac{9 \times \cancel{7}}{\cancel{7}} \\\\
a                                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of tables}                 &= 9 \\\\
\text{Numbers of stools}                &= a + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 9 + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9,12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of tables $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of stools $= a + 3$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a + 3 \times (a + 3)           &= 72 \\\\
4a + 3a + 9                             &= 72 \\\\
7a + 9                                  &= 72 \\\\
7a                                      &= 72 - 9 \\\\
7a                                      &= 63 \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac{63}{7} \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac{9 \times 7}{7} \\\\
a                                       &= \dfrac{9 \times \cancel{7}}{\cancel{7}} \\\\
a                                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of tables}                 &= 9 \\\\
\text{Numbers of stools}                &= a + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 9 + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9,12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-1:1:24</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Adam, Ben and Carl are weighing themselves.

Together, Adam and Ben weigh $102 \ \text{kg}$.

Ben and Carl weigh $97 \ \text{kg}$.

Adam and Carl weigh $93 \ \text{kg}$.

What is their total weight in kg? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of Adam $= a$

Let the weight of Ben $= b$

Let the weight of Carl $= c$

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                               &= 102 \\\\
b                                   &= 102 - a \\\\
b + c                               &= 97 \\\\
102 - a + c                         &= 97 \\\\
{}- a + c                           &= 97 - 102 \\\\
{}-a + c                            &= -5 \\\\
c                                   &= -5+a \\\\
a + c                               &= 93 \\\\
a - 5 + a                           &= 93 \\\\
2a                                  &= 93 + 5 \\\\
2a                                  &= 98 \\\\
a                                   & = \dfrac{98}{2} \\\\
a                                   & = \dfrac{49 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                                   & = \dfrac{49 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                   &= 49 \\\\
b                                   &= 53 \\\\
c                                   &= 45 \\\\
\text{Total Weight}                 &= a + b + c \\\\
                                    &= 49 + 53 + 45 \\\\
                                    &= 147 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$  

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$147$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of Adam $= a$
<emptyline>
Let the weight of Ben $= b$
<emptyline>
Let the weight of Carl $= c$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                               &= 102 \\\\
b                                   &= 102 - a \\\\
b + c                               &= 97 \\\\
102 - a + c                         &= 97 \\\\
{}- a + c                           &= 97 - 102 \\\\
{}-a + c                            &= -5 \\\\
c                                   &= -5+a \\\\
a + c                               &= 93 \\\\
a - 5 + a                           &= 93 \\\\
2a                                  &= 93 + 5 \\\\
2a                                  &= 98 \\\\
a                                   & = \dfrac{98}{2} \\\\
a                                   & = \dfrac{49 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                                   & = \dfrac{49 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                   &= 49 \\\\
b                                   &= 53 \\\\
c                                   &= 45 \\\\
\text{Total Weight}                 &= a + b + c \\\\
                                    &= 49 + 53 + 45 \\\\
                                    &= 147 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$  
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$147$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

$a$ and $b$ are two *different* positive whole numbers which make the following statement true

$3a + 2b = 30$ 

Find two possible pairs of numbers which make the statement true.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

For $a =10$,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 10 + 2b                    &= 30 \\\\
30 + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 0 \\\\
b                                   &= 0
\end{aligned}
$

For $a= 2$,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 2 + 2b                     &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 6 \\\\
2b                                  &= 24 \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {24} {2} \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b                                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$a = 10,b = 0 \\
a = 2,b = 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>For $a =10$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 10 + 2b                    &= 30 \\\\
30 + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 0 \\\\
b                                   &= 0
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
For $a= 2$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 2 + 2b                     &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 6 \\\\
2b                                  &= 24 \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {24} {2} \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b                                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$a = 10,b = 0 \\
a = 2,b = 12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

$p$ and $q$ are whole numbers. $p$ is $150$ greater than $q$.

The following statement is also true
                       
$\large p + q = 1000$

Calculate the numbers $p$ and $q$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$p = 150 + q$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
p + q         &= 1000 \\\\
150 + q + q   &= 1000 \\\\
150 + 2 q     &= 1000 \\\\
2 q           &= 1000 - 150 \\\\
2 q           &= 850 \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {850} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times 2} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
q             &= 425
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
p  &= 150 + q \\\\
p  &= 150 + 425 \\\\
p  &= 575
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$p = 575, q= 425$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$p = 150 + q$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
p + q         &= 1000 \\\\
150 + q + q   &= 1000 \\\\
150 + 2 q     &= 1000 \\\\
2 q           &= 1000 - 150 \\\\
2 q           &= 850 \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {850} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times 2} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
q             &= 425
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
p  &= 150 + q \\\\
p  &= 150 + 425 \\\\
p  &= 575
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$p = 575, q= 425$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Two workers are paid a total of $\pounds 506$ by their employer. If one is paid $20\%$ more than the other, how much do they each earn? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the wage of first labourer $= a$

Wage of second labourer $= a + 20 \% \times a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 20 \% \times a                                                      &= 506 \\\\
2a + \dfrac{20}{100} \times a                                               &= 506 \\\\
2a + \dfrac{20 \times a}{100}                                               &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{2a \times 100}{1 \times 100} + \dfrac{20a \times 1}{100 \times 1}    &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac {2a \times 100 + 20a} {100}                                          &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{200a + 20a}{100}                                                     &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{220a}{100}                                                           &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{220a}{100}                                                           &= 506 \\\\
220a                                                                        &= 506 \times 100 \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{506 \times 100}{220} \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{506 \times 10 \times \cancel{10}}{22 \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{506 \times 5 \times \cancel{2}}{11 \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{46 \times \cancel{11} \times 5}{\cancel{11}} \\\\
a                                                                           &= 46 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                                           &= \pounds 230 \\\\
\text{Wage of first worker}                                                 &= \pounds 230 \\\\
\text{Wage of second worker}                                                &= 506 - 230 \\\\
                                                                            &= \pounds 276
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 230, \ \pounds 276$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the wage of first labourer $= a$
<emptyline>
Wage of second labourer $= a + 20 \% \times a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 20 \% \times a                                                      &= 506 \\\\
2a + \dfrac{20}{100} \times a                                               &= 506 \\\\
2a + \dfrac{20 \times a}{100}                                               &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{2a \times 100}{1 \times 100} + \dfrac{20a \times 1}{100 \times 1}    &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac {2a \times 100 + 20a} {100}                                          &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{200a + 20a}{100}                                                     &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{220a}{100}                                                           &= 506 \\\\
\dfrac{220a}{100}                                                           &= 506 \\\\
220a                                                                        &= 506 \times 100 \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{506 \times 100}{220} \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{506 \times 10 \times \cancel{10}}{22 \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{506 \times 5 \times \cancel{2}}{11 \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                                           &= \dfrac{46 \times \cancel{11} \times 5}{\cancel{11}} \\\\
a                                                                           &= 46 \times 5 \\\\
a                                                                           &= \pounds 230 \\\\
\text{Wage of first worker}                                                 &= \pounds 230 \\\\
\text{Wage of second worker}                                                &= 506 - 230 \\\\
                                                                            &= \pounds 276
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 230, \ \pounds 276$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>

Let the missing number $=a$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
28 \times a &= 5600 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {5600} {28} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {200 \times 28} {28} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {200 \times \cancel {28}} {\cancel {28}} \\\\
a           &= 200
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2100 \times a &= 84000 \\\\
a             &= \dfrac {84000} {2100} \\\\
a             &= \dfrac {21 \times 4 \times 1000} {21 \times 100} \\\\
a             &= \dfrac {\cancel {21} \times 4 \times 10 \cancel {00}} {\cancel {21} \times 1 \cancel {00}} \\\\
a             &= 4 \times 10 \\\\
a             &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Albert has five times as many apples as Bertie. If Albert gives Bertie $26$ apples, they will each have the same number of apples.

How many apples do they have in total between them? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of apples Bertie has $= a$

Number of apples Albert has $= 5a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5a - 26                         &= a + 26 \\\\
5a - a                          &= 26 + 26 \\\\
4a                              &= 52 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{52}{4} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{13 \times 4}{4} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{13 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
a                               &= 13 \\\\
\text{Total number of apples}   &= a + 5a \\\\
                                &= 6a \\\\
                                &= 6 \times 13 \\\\
                                &= 78 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$78$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of apples Bertie has $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of apples Albert has $= 5a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5a - 26                         &= a + 26 \\\\
5a - a                          &= 26 + 26 \\\\
4a                              &= 52 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{52}{4} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{13 \times 4}{4} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac{13 \times \cancel{4}}{\cancel{4}} \\\\
a                               &= 13 \\\\
\text{Total number of apples}   &= a + 5a \\\\
                                &= 6a \\\\
                                &= 6 \times 13 \\\\
                                &= 78 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$78$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I think of a number, add five and then divide by two. My answer is $-19$.

What number was I thinking of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 5) \div 2                  &= -19 \\\\
\dfrac{(a + 5)}{2}              &= -19 \\\\
a + 5                           &= -19 \times 2 \\\\
a + 5                           &= -38 \\\\
a                               &= -38 - 5 \\\\
a                               &= -43
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-43$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(a + 5) \div 2                  &= -19 \\\\
\dfrac{(a + 5)}{2}              &= -19 \\\\
a + 5                           &= -19 \times 2 \\\\
a + 5                           &= -38 \\\\
a                               &= -38 - 5 \\\\
a                               &= -43
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Twice a number added to half of the same number gives $250$.

What was the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 a + \dfrac{a} {2}                                &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac {2 a \times 2} {1 \times 2} + \dfrac{a} {2} &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac{4 a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {2}                   &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac{4 a + a} {2}                                &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac{5a} {2}                                     &= 250 \\\\
a                                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 5 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {5} \times 2} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                                                  &= 50 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 a + \dfrac{a} {2}                                &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac {2 a \times 2} {1 \times 2} + \dfrac{a} {2} &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac{4 a} {2} + \dfrac {a} {2}                   &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac{4 a + a} {2}                                &= 250 \\\\
\dfrac{5a} {2}                                     &= 250 \\\\
a                                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 5 \times 2} {5} \\\\
a                                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {5} \times 2} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                                                  &= 50 \times 2 \\\\
a                                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When eighteen is added to twice the square of a number, the result is $50$.

What are the two possible starting numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
18 + 2(a ^ 2)                   &= 50 \\\\
2(a ^ 2)                        &= 50 - 18 \\\\
2(a ^ 2)                        &= 32 \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {32} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= 16 \\\\
a                               &= \sqrt{16} \\\\
a                               &= + 4 \ \text{and} \ -4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$+ 4, \ -4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>

a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a ^ 2                           &= 16 \\\\
a                               &= \sqrt{16} \\\\
a                               &= + 4 \ \text{and} \ -4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$+ 4, \ -4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When three tenths of a number is subtracted from ninety-five hundreds of the same number, the result is $1.95$.

What was the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3 a} {10} - \dfrac {90 a} {500}                      &= 1.95 \\\\
\dfrac {3 a \times 50} {10 \times 50} - \dfrac {90 a} {500}  &= 1.95 \\\\
\dfrac {150 a} {500} - \dfrac {90 a} {500}                   &= 1.95 \\\\
\dfrac {150 a - 90a} {500}                                   &= 1.95 \\\\
\dfrac {60 a} {500}                                          &= 1.95 \\\\
a                                                            &= \dfrac {1.95 \times 500} {60} \\\\
a                                                            &= \dfrac {975} {60} \\\\
a                                                            &= 16.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<emptyline>

$

\dfrac {3 a \times 50} {10 \times 50} - \dfrac {90 a} {500}  &= 1.95 \\\\
\dfrac {150 a} {500} - \dfrac {90 a} {500}                   &= 1.95 \\\\
\dfrac {150 a - 90a} {500}                                   &= 1.95 \\\\
\dfrac {60 a} {500}                                          &= 1.95 \\\\
a                                                            &= \dfrac {1.95 \times 500} {60} \\\\
a                                                            &= \dfrac {975} {60} \\\\
a                                                            &= 16.25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

An iPad used to cost $\pounds 330$. Since Brexit the price has increased by $22\%$.

How much does it cost now? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

If the cost of iPad increased by $22 \%$, then the new height will be $100 + 22 = 122 \%$ of the previous cost. Using that, we get

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {New cost of iPad}              &= \dfrac {122} {100} \times 330 \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {122 \times 330} {100} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {122 \times 33\cancel{0}} {10\cancel{0}} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{122 \times 33}{10} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{4026}{10} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 402.6
\end {aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$402.6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>If the cost of iPad increased by $22 \%$, then the new height will be $100 + 22 = 122 \%$ of the previous cost. Using that, we get
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {New cost of iPad}              &= \dfrac {122} {100} \times 330 \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {122 \times 330} {100} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {122 \times 33\cancel{0}} {10\cancel{0}} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{122 \times 33}{10} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{4026}{10} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 402.6
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$402.6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

$a$ and $b$ are two *different, positive* whole numbers which make the following statement true

$4a + 3b = 48$


Find two possible pairs of numbers which make the statement above true.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's try for $a =12$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 12 + 3b                                &= 48 \\\\
48 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 48 \\\\
b                                               &= 0
\end{aligned}
$

Let's try for $a =3$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 3 + 3b                                 &= 48 \\\\
12 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 12 \\\\
3b                                              &= 36 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
b                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$a=12, b=0 \\ a=3, b=12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's try for $a =12$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 12 + 3b                                &= 48 \\\\
48 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 48 \\\\
b                                               &= 0
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's try for $a =3$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 3 + 3b                                 &= 48 \\\\
12 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 12 \\\\
3b                                              &= 36 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
b                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$a=12, b=0 \\ a=3, b=12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A large sack holds $20\%$ more potatoes than a small sack. If one large sack and one small sack together contain $77$ potatoes, how many potatoes does each sack hold?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the potatoes in small sack $= a$

Potatoes in large sack $= a + 20 \% \times a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 20 \% \times a                              &= 77 \\\\
2a + \dfrac{20}{100} \times a                       &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac{2a \times 100}{100} + \dfrac{20a}{100}       &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac {2a \times 100 + 20a} {100}                  &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac{200a + 20a}{100}                             &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac{220a}{100}                                   &= 77 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac{77 \times 100}{220} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{11} \times \cancel{2}\times 5 \times \cancel{10}} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{11} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 35 \\\\
\text{Potatoes in large sack}                       &= 77 - a \\\\
                                                    &= 77 - 35 \\\\
                                                    &= 42
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35,42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the potatoes in small sack $= a$
<emptyline>
Potatoes in large sack $= a + 20 \% \times a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 20 \% \times a                              &= 77 \\\\
2a + \dfrac{20}{100} \times a                       &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac{2a \times 100}{100} + \dfrac{20a}{100}       &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac {2a \times 100 + 20a} {100}                  &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac{200a + 20a}{100}                             &= 77 \\\\
\dfrac{220a}{100}                                   &= 77 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac{77 \times 100}{220} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{11} \times \cancel{2}\times 5 \times \cancel{10}} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{11} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
a                                                   &= 35 \\\\
\text{Potatoes in large sack}                       &= 77 - a \\\\
                                                    &= 77 - 35 \\\\
                                                    &= 42
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35,42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Let missing number = a

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
37 \times a &= 7400 \\\\

a   &= \dfrac{200 \times 37}{37} \\\\
a   &= \dfrac{200 \times \cancel{37}}{\cancel{37}} \\\\
a   &= 200
\end{aligned}
$

I think of a number, then subtract fifteen. The result is thirty-seven.

What was the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 15                                                  &= 37 \\\\
a                                                       &= 37 + 15 \\\\
a                                                       &= 52 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<emptyline>

$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

I think of a number, multiply it by six, then subtract nine. The result is thirty-three. 

What was the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 6 - 9                                      &= 33 \\\\
a \times 6                                          &= 33 + 9 \\\\
a \times 6                                          &= 42
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I think of a number, double it, then add fifteen. The result is three. 

What was the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $=a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 15                                         &= 3 \\\\
2a                                                      &= 3 - 15 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{-12}{2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{-6 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{-6 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                       &= -6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $=a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$

2a                                                      &= 3 - 15 \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{-12}{2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{-6 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a                                                       &= \dfrac{-6 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}} \\\\
a                                                       &= -6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$-6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Sophia did push-ups every day for $7$ days. Each day after the first day, she did $5$ more push-ups than the day before. In total she did $175$ push-ups. How many push-ups did Sophia do on the last day?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the number of pushups on firstday $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 5 + a + 10 + a + 15 + ....                                      &= 175 \\\\
(a + a + a + a + a + a + a) + (5 + 10 + 15 + 20 + 25 + 30)              &= 175 \\\\
7a + 105                                                                &= 175 \\\\
7a                                                                      &= 175 - 105 \\\\
7a                                                                      &= 70 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 10 \\\\
\text{Number of pushups on last day}                                    &= a + 30 \\\\
                                                                        &= 10 + 30 \\\\
                                                                        &= 40 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the number of pushups on firstday $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 5 + a + 10 + a + 15 + ....                                      &= 175 \\\\
(a + a + a + a + a + a + a) + (5 + 10 + 15 + 20 + 25 + 30)              &= 175 \\\\
7a + 105                                                                &= 175 \\\\
7a                                                                      &= 175 - 105 \\\\
7a                                                                      &= 70 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 10 \\\\
\text{Number of pushups on last day}                                    &= a + 30 \\\\
                                                                        &= 10 + 30 \\\\
                                                                        &= 40 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The mean (average) of a set of six numbers is $10$. The number $25$ is removed from the set.

Calculate the mean of the remaining numbers. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of 6 numbers}               &= 10 \times 6 \\\\
                                      &= 60 \\\\
\text{Mean of the remaining numbers}  &= \dfrac{60 - 25}{5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{35}{5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{7 \times 5}{5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
                                      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of 6 numbers}               &= 10 \times 6 \\\\
                                      &= 60 \\\\
\text{Mean of the remaining numbers}  &= \dfrac{60 - 25}{5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{35}{5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{7 \times 5}{5} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac{7 \times \cancel{5}}{\cancel{5}} \\\\
                                      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
